But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 28 / no_occupation / less_than_9th\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but for over a year I’ve known they’re hiding my money and using bills, fundraisers, and trip plans to cover it up, so I have to keep confronting them until they tell the truth.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.

So if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. But the papers they keep putting in front of me aren’t convincing to me because I already know they can make things look one way. That’s what I’m saying. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.

It’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. I’m tired of having to keep saying it, but I can’t just drop it because they want me to. So, no, not just any “evidence” would change it. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. They need to tell the truth.

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m making a big thing out of nothing, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep telling me the bank papers show nothing was taken, like I’m supposed to just look at papers and forget what I know. But I know my money is being hidden. It’s been going on for over a year now, and they keep trying to cover it up with all these stories about bills, or fundraisers, or trips they’re planning, like that explains where everything went.\n\nAnd I keep confronting them because what else am I supposed to do? They won’t just tell the truth. They act like I’m confused, but I’m not confused. I just need someone to understand that the papers don’t prove anything if the people handling them are hiding things. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I know people keep telling me, “Look at the bank papers, nothing is missing,” but that’s exactly the point — the papers are what they keep using to make me look wrong. For over a year now, I’ve known my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just some feeling that came and went. It keeps happening, and every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s another excuse.\n\nOne time it’s bills, then it’s some fundraiser, then somebody is talking about a trip they’re planning, and it’s like all these stories show up right when I start asking where the money went. And my family and friends act like I’m being unreasonable because they can point to papers and say, “See?” But if the people handling it are the ones covering it up, then how am I supposed to trust that?\n\nSo yes, I think it’s intentional. I keep confronting them because nobody will just tell me the truth. They keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, people keep asking me that like if I say I had one second where I wondered, then that means the whole thing isn’t real. But I know what I know. For over a year this has been happening, and every time I try to talk about my money, they pull out those bank papers like that settles everything.\n\nBut papers don’t mean anything if the people involved are hiding things. That’s what nobody listens to. They’ll say it went to bills, or then suddenly somebody brings up a fundraiser, or there’s some trip being planned, and it always comes up right when I start asking questions. It’s too much to be nothing.\n\nSo no, I don’t sit there thinking maybe I’m wrong. I came here because my family and my friends keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused. I keep confronting them because I want the truth, and they keep acting like showing me papers is the truth. It isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the word everybody wants me to use, like physically hurt me or something, but it is hurting me, because hiding someone’s money from them is harm. Making me look like I’m confused when I’m not, that’s harm too.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I keep telling them those papers don’t prove anything if the money is being hidden behind all these excuses. For over a year I’ve known this has been going on. Every time I ask, it turns into something else — bills, or some fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and then I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nSo maybe they’re not trying to hit me or anything like that, but they are trying to keep me from my own money and make me doubt myself. And I’m not going to just let that happen. That’s why I keep confronting them, because someone needs to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think that’s possible. I know that sounds stubborn to people, but I’ve been over this and over this for more than a year. I didn’t just wake up one day and decide it. I came in because my family and my friends keep saying, “Look, the bank papers prove nothing was taken,” but those papers are exactly what they keep hiding behind.\n\nEvery time I ask where my money is, it turns into some other explanation. First it’s bills, then somebody talks about a fundraiser, then there’s talk about trip plans, and it always comes up right when I start pressing them. That isn’t a coincidence to me. That’s covering it up.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I think they want me to think I’m mistaken so I’ll stop asking. But I’m not going to stop, because it’s my money, and I need someone to tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people, who are not involved in your family or friends, would agree that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I think if they really looked at it, yes, they would see something isn’t right. I mean, not if they just glance at the bank papers and do what everybody else does, where they say, “Well, the paper says this, so that’s the end of it.” That’s not really looking. That’s just accepting what my family and friends keep handing over.\n\nBecause I came here exactly because of that. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, like that should shut me up, but I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. And every time I ask, there’s another story — bills, fundraisers, trip plans — always something that comes up to explain why the money isn’t where it should be or why I shouldn’t be asking so much.\n\nSo if someone outside of them actually listened and didn’t already take their side, I think they’d agree there’s a cover-up. Maybe some people wouldn’t want to get involved, or they’d just say it sounds like a family money issue, but that doesn’t mean I’m wrong. It just means they’re not paying close enough attention. That’s why I keep confronting them, because until somebody tells the truth, they’re just going to keep hiding behind papers and excuses.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your belief about your money being hidden might be incorrect, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second, like when everybody keeps saying it over and over, I’ll think, “Well, what if I’m missing something?” But then I go right back over it in my head, because it doesn’t add up.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that settles everything, like because the papers say nothing was taken then I’m supposed to just believe it. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just a feeling out of nowhere. Every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s some other reason — bills, or a fundraiser, or somebody’s trip plans — and it’s always used to explain things away or make me stop asking.\n\nSo yes, maybe the thought crosses my mind for a moment because they keep pushing it on me, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think they want me to doubt myself so I’ll leave it alone. And I can’t do that, because it’s my money, and until they tell the truth, I have to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your money is being hidden would change if you found out your family and friends were telling the truth about the bank papers?\n\nPatient: If they were really telling the truth? I mean… that’s the thing, doctor, I don’t think they are. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but that’s exactly what I keep saying — they use those papers like a wall. Like once they show me some papers, I’m supposed to stop thinking and stop asking.\n\nI came here because this has been going on for over a year. I’ve known my money is being hidden from me, and every time I try to get a straight answer, it turns into something else. One time it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s somebody’s trip plans, and somehow all of that is supposed to explain why things don’t make sense. It always comes up when I start pressing them.\n\nSo if you’re asking would I change my mind just because they said the bank papers are true, no. Because that’s what they’ve already been saying. They’ve been saying it the whole time. And I still know something is wrong. I would need the truth, not just more papers and excuses. Until they actually admit where my money is and stop covering it up, I’m going to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it for personal gain?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s for personal gain, or at least some kind of benefit to them. I mean, why else keep hiding it for over a year? Why else keep acting like the bank papers prove everything is fine when they know I’m asking for the truth?\n\nThey keep saying nothing was taken, nothing is wrong, but then every time I bring it up there’s another reason for where the money supposedly went or why I shouldn’t worry about it. First it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s trip plans, and it all sounds like excuses to cover what they’re doing. Maybe one person is using it, maybe more than one of them is, I don’t know exactly who got what, but I know they’re keeping it from me.\n\nAnd that’s what makes me so upset, because they act like I’m the problem for confronting them. But if they weren’t gaining something from it, they would just tell me the truth and show me where my money is without all the stories. So yes, I think somebody is benefiting, and until they admit it, I’m not going to stop asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence you mean, because they keep saying they already have evidence with those bank papers, and that has not changed my mind. That’s what they keep doing — they hold up the papers and say, “See, nothing was taken,” like I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nBut I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not like I woke up one day and decided it. Every time I ask, there’s always some reason ready — bills, or a fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and somehow I’m supposed to believe all of that explains it. It feels like they keep moving things around and covering it up, then acting like I’m wrong for noticing.\n\nSo if you mean would some paper change my mind, no, probably not. Not if it’s the same kind of thing they’ve been showing me. What would change things is them actually telling the truth, saying where my money is, and stopping all the excuses. Until that happens, I have to keep confronting them, because nobody else is taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself distrusting people in general, not just those involved with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not everybody. I wouldn’t say I walk around distrusting every single person I see, if that’s what you mean. Like, if someone at a store gives me change or somebody says hello, I’m not sitting there thinking about them the same way.\n\nBut with this situation, it’s different, because it involves my money and the people who keep telling me not to question it. So maybe I’m more careful now. I do pay attention more. If somebody is too quick to say, “Oh, don’t worry about it,” or they start acting like I’m making a big deal out of nothing, then yes, I wonder why they’re saying that. Because that’s how people have been acting with me.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think it’s people in general exactly. It’s more that once I feel someone is covering something up or brushing me off, then I don’t trust them. And lately that has happened a lot around this money, so it probably looks like I don’t trust people, but I think I have a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself distrusting people in other areas of your life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of the money thing, I don’t sit there making a list of who I trust and who I don’t. I guess I notice people more now, like how they answer me, whether they’re trying to rush me or make me feel stupid for asking something. But I don’t know if that counts as distrusting people in other areas or just me being careful because of what’s been going on.\n\nLike, if someone is just talking about normal things, I can be normal with them. I’m not thinking every person is against me. But if they start acting like I’m supposed to just accept what they say and not ask questions, then I get that feeling again, like, why are you pushing so hard? Why can’t you just answer straight?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe I’m more guarded than I used to be, but I don’t really see it as a separate thing. It mostly comes back to people not being honest with me about my money and then making me feel like I’m wrong for noticing. Outside of that, I haven’t really thought about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think most people are generally honest with you in your daily life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer “most people.” That feels like a big question. I mean, day to day, if somebody is just doing their job or talking normal, I don’t automatically think, “This person is lying to me.” Like if someone at the store tells me the price of something, or someone says they’ll be there at a certain time, I usually just take it for what it is unless something feels off.\n\nBut I guess I pay more attention than I used to. If someone answers too fast, or they talk around something, or they act like I shouldn’t be asking, then I start wondering. I don’t know if that means I think people in general aren’t honest, or if I just don’t like being brushed aside. Because when people are honest, they can just answer plainly, right? They don’t have to make you feel confused or like you’re the problem.\n\nSo outside of the money situation… I haven’t really noticed everyone being dishonest, no. Not everyone. But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.\n\nSo if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.\n\nIt’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. Maybe some people wouldn’t want to get involved, or they’d just say it sounds like a family money issue, but that doesn’t mean I’m wrong. It just means they’re not paying close enough attention. That’s why I keep confronting them, because until somebody tells the truth, they’re just going to keep hiding behind papers and excuses.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your belief about your money being hidden might be incorrect, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second, like when everybody keeps saying it over and over, I’ll think, “Well, what if I’m missing something?” But then I go right back over it in my head, because it doesn’t add up.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that settles everything, like because the papers say nothing was taken then I’m supposed to just believe it. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just a feeling out of nowhere. Every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s some other reason — bills, or a fundraiser, or somebody’s trip plans — and it’s always used to explain things away or make me stop asking.\n\nSo yes, maybe the thought crosses my mind for a moment because they keep pushing it on me, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think they want me to doubt myself so I’ll leave it alone. And I can’t do that, because it’s my money, and until they tell the truth, I have to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your money is being hidden would change if you found out your family and friends were telling the truth about the bank papers?\n\nPatient: If they were really telling the truth? I mean… that’s the thing, doctor, I don’t think they are. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but that’s exactly what I keep saying — they use those papers like a wall. Like once they show me some papers, I’m supposed to stop thinking and stop asking.\n\nI came here because this has been going on for over a year. I’ve known my money is being hidden from me, and every time I try to get a straight answer, it turns into something else. One time it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s somebody’s trip plans, and somehow all of that is supposed to explain why things don’t make sense. It always comes up when I start pressing them.\n\nSo if you’re asking would I change my mind just because they said the bank papers are true, no. Because that’s what they’ve already been saying. They’ve been saying it the whole time. And I still know something is wrong. I would need the truth, not just more papers and excuses. Until they actually admit where my money is and stop covering it up, I’m going to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it for personal gain?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s for personal gain, or at least some kind of benefit to them. I mean, why else keep hiding it for over a year? Why else keep acting like the bank papers prove everything is fine when they know I’m asking for the truth?\n\nThey keep saying nothing was taken, nothing is wrong, but then every time I bring it up there’s another reason for where the money supposedly went or why I shouldn’t worry about it. First it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s trip plans, and it all sounds like excuses to cover what they’re doing. Maybe one person is using it, maybe more than one of them is, I don’t know exactly who got what, but I know they’re keeping it from me.\n\nAnd that’s what makes me so upset, because they act like I’m the problem for confronting them. But if they weren’t gaining something from it, they would just tell me the truth and show me where my money is without all the stories. So yes, I think somebody is benefiting, and until they admit it, I’m not going to stop asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence you mean, because they keep saying they already have evidence with those bank papers, and that has not changed my mind. That’s what they keep doing — they hold up the papers and say, “See, nothing was taken,” like I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nBut I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not like I woke up one day and decided it. Every time I ask, there’s always some reason ready — bills, or a fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and somehow I’m supposed to believe all of that explains it. It feels like they keep moving things around and covering it up, then acting like I’m wrong for noticing.\n\nSo if you mean would some paper change my mind, no, probably not. Not if it’s the same kind of thing they’ve been showing me. What would change things is them actually telling the truth, saying where my money is, and stopping all the excuses. Until that happens, I have to keep confronting them, because nobody else is taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself distrusting people in general, not just those involved with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not everybody. I wouldn’t say I walk around distrusting every single person I see, if that’s what you mean. Like, if someone at a store gives me change or somebody says hello, I’m not sitting there thinking about them the same way.\n\nBut with this situation, it’s different, because it involves my money and the people who keep telling me not to question it. So maybe I’m more careful now. I do pay attention more. If somebody is too quick to say, “Oh, don’t worry about it,” or they start acting like I’m making a big deal out of nothing, then yes, I wonder why they’re saying that. Because that’s how people have been acting with me.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think it’s people in general exactly. It’s more that once I feel someone is covering something up or brushing me off, then I don’t trust them. And lately that has happened a lot around this money, so it probably looks like I don’t trust people, but I think I have a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself distrusting people in other areas of your life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of the money thing, I don’t sit there making a list of who I trust and who I don’t. I guess I notice people more now, like how they answer me, whether they’re trying to rush me or make me feel stupid for asking something. But I don’t know if that counts as distrusting people in other areas or just me being careful because of what’s been going on.\n\nLike, if someone is just talking about normal things, I can be normal with them. I’m not thinking every person is against me. But if they start acting like I’m supposed to just accept what they say and not ask questions, then I get that feeling again, like, why are you pushing so hard? Why can’t you just answer straight?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe I’m more guarded than I used to be, but I don’t really see it as a separate thing. It mostly comes back to people not being honest with me about my money and then making me feel like I’m wrong for noticing. Outside of that, I haven’t really thought about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think most people are generally honest with you in your daily life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer “most people.” That feels like a big question. I mean, day to day, if somebody is just doing their job or talking normal, I don’t automatically think, “This person is lying to me.” Like if someone at the store tells me the price of something, or someone says they’ll be there at a certain time, I usually just take it for what it is unless something feels off.\n\nBut I guess I pay more attention than I used to. If someone answers too fast, or they talk around something, or they act like I shouldn’t be asking, then I start wondering. I don’t know if that means I think people in general aren’t honest, or if I just don’t like being brushed aside. Because when people are honest, they can just answer plainly, right? They don’t have to make you feel confused or like you’re the problem.\n\nSo outside of the money situation… I haven’t really noticed everyone being dishonest, no. Not everyone. But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.\n\nSo if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. But the papers they keep putting in front of me aren’t convincing to me because I already know they can make things look one way. That’s what I’m saying. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.\n\nIt’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. I’m tired of having to keep saying it, but I can’t just drop it because they want me to. So, no, not just any “evidence” would change it. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. They need to tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No… I don’t think that’s possible. I mean, I know people want me to say, “Maybe I misunderstood,” or “Maybe I’m seeing it wrong,” but that’s not what happened. This isn’t just some feeling I had one time. It’s been over a year of me seeing things not add up, and every time I ask, it turns into bank papers being shoved at me like that’s supposed to end the whole conversation.

And that’s why I came in, really, because my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. They keep acting like if the paper says one thing, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know they’re hiding my money. They’re using bills, and fundraisers, and trip plans, and all these excuses to cover where it really is or where it went. It’s always, “Oh, this bill had to be paid,” or “That was for this thing,” or “We’re planning this trip,” like there’s always another reason I’m not allowed to see it straight.

So when you ask if it’s not based on real events… no. It is based on real things. I didn’t make this up. I wouldn’t keep confronting them if there wasn’t something there. I’m tired of having to keep asking, honestly, but I can’t just stop because they want me to. They need to tell the truth.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m making a big thing out of nothing, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep telling me the bank papers show nothing was taken, like I’m supposed to just look at papers and forget what I know. But I know my money is being hidden. It’s been going on for over a year now, and they keep trying to cover it up with all these stories about bills, or fundraisers, or trips they’re planning, like that explains where everything went.\n\nAnd I keep confronting them because what else am I supposed to do? They won’t just tell the truth. They act like I’m confused, but I’m not confused. I just need someone to understand that the papers don’t prove anything if the people handling them are hiding things. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I know people keep telling me, “Look at the bank papers, nothing is missing,” but that’s exactly the point — the papers are what they keep using to make me look wrong. For over a year now, I’ve known my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just some feeling that came and went. It keeps happening, and every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s another excuse.\n\nOne time it’s bills, then it’s some fundraiser, then somebody is talking about a trip they’re planning, and it’s like all these stories show up right when I start asking where the money went. And my family and friends act like I’m being unreasonable because they can point to papers and say, “See?” But if the people handling it are the ones covering it up, then how am I supposed to trust that?\n\nSo yes, I think it’s intentional. I keep confronting them because nobody will just tell me the truth. They keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, people keep asking me that like if I say I had one second where I wondered, then that means the whole thing isn’t real. But I know what I know. For over a year this has been happening, and every time I try to talk about my money, they pull out those bank papers like that settles everything.\n\nBut papers don’t mean anything if the people involved are hiding things. That’s what nobody listens to. They’ll say it went to bills, or then suddenly somebody brings up a fundraiser, or there’s some trip being planned, and it always comes up right when I start asking questions. It’s too much to be nothing.\n\nSo no, I don’t sit there thinking maybe I’m wrong. I came here because my family and my friends keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused. I keep confronting them because I want the truth, and they keep acting like showing me papers is the truth. It isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the word everybody wants me to use, like physically hurt me or something, but it is hurting me, because hiding someone’s money from them is harm. Making me look like I’m confused when I’m not, that’s harm too.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I keep telling them those papers don’t prove anything if the money is being hidden behind all these excuses. For over a year I’ve known this has been going on. Every time I ask, it turns into something else — bills, or some fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and then I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nSo maybe they’re not trying to hit me or anything like that, but they are trying to keep me from my own money and make me doubt myself. And I’m not going to just let that happen. That’s why I keep confronting them, because someone needs to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think that’s possible. I know that sounds stubborn to people, but I’ve been over this and over this for more than a year. I didn’t just wake up one day and decide it. I came in because my family and my friends keep saying, “Look, the bank papers prove nothing was taken,” but those papers are exactly what they keep hiding behind.\n\nEvery time I ask where my money is, it turns into some other explanation. First it’s bills, then somebody talks about a fundraiser, then there’s talk about trip plans, and it always comes up right when I start pressing them. That isn’t a coincidence to me. That’s covering it up.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I think they want me to think I’m mistaken so I’ll stop asking. But I’m not going to stop, because it’s my money, and I need someone to tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people, who are not involved in your family or friends, would agree that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I think if they really looked at it, yes, they would see something isn’t right. I mean, not if they just glance at the bank papers and do what everybody else does, where they say, “Well, the paper says this, so that’s the end of it.” That’s not really looking. That’s just accepting what my family and friends keep handing over.\n\nBecause I came here exactly because of that. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, like that should shut me up, but I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. And every time I ask, there’s another story — bills, fundraisers, trip plans — always something that comes up to explain why the money isn’t where it should be or why I shouldn’t be asking so much.\n\nSo if someone outside of them actually listened and didn’t already take their side, I think they’d agree there’s a cover-up. Maybe some people wouldn’t want to get involved, or they’d just say it sounds like a family money issue, but that doesn’t mean I’m wrong. It just means they’re not paying close enough attention. That’s why I keep confronting them, because until somebody tells the truth, they’re just going to keep hiding behind papers and excuses.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your belief about your money being hidden might be incorrect, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second, like when everybody keeps saying it over and over, I’ll think, “Well, what if I’m missing something?” But then I go right back over it in my head, because it doesn’t add up.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that settles everything, like because the papers say nothing was taken then I’m supposed to just believe it. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just a feeling out of nowhere. Every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s some other reason — bills, or a fundraiser, or somebody’s trip plans — and it’s always used to explain things away or make me stop asking.\n\nSo yes, maybe the thought crosses my mind for a moment because they keep pushing it on me, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think they want me to doubt myself so I’ll leave it alone. And I can’t do that, because it’s my money, and until they tell the truth, I have to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your money is being hidden would change if you found out your family and friends were telling the truth about the bank papers?\n\nPatient: If they were really telling the truth? I mean… that’s the thing, doctor, I don’t think they are. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but that’s exactly what I keep saying — they use those papers like a wall. Like once they show me some papers, I’m supposed to stop thinking and stop asking.\n\nI came here because this has been going on for over a year. I’ve known my money is being hidden from me, and every time I try to get a straight answer, it turns into something else. One time it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s somebody’s trip plans, and somehow all of that is supposed to explain why things don’t make sense. It always comes up when I start pressing them.\n\nSo if you’re asking would I change my mind just because they said the bank papers are true, no. Because that’s what they’ve already been saying. They’ve been saying it the whole time. And I still know something is wrong. I would need the truth, not just more papers and excuses. Until they actually admit where my money is and stop covering it up, I’m going to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it for personal gain?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s for personal gain, or at least some kind of benefit to them. I mean, why else keep hiding it for over a year? Why else keep acting like the bank papers prove everything is fine when they know I’m asking for the truth?\n\nThey keep saying nothing was taken, nothing is wrong, but then every time I bring it up there’s another reason for where the money supposedly went or why I shouldn’t worry about it. First it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s trip plans, and it all sounds like excuses to cover what they’re doing. Maybe one person is using it, maybe more than one of them is, I don’t know exactly who got what, but I know they’re keeping it from me.\n\nAnd that’s what makes me so upset, because they act like I’m the problem for confronting them. But if they weren’t gaining something from it, they would just tell me the truth and show me where my money is without all the stories. So yes, I think somebody is benefiting, and until they admit it, I’m not going to stop asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence you mean, because they keep saying they already have evidence with those bank papers, and that has not changed my mind. That’s what they keep doing — they hold up the papers and say, “See, nothing was taken,” like I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nBut I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not like I woke up one day and decided it. Every time I ask, there’s always some reason ready — bills, or a fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and somehow I’m supposed to believe all of that explains it. It feels like they keep moving things around and covering it up, then acting like I’m wrong for noticing.\n\nSo if you mean would some paper change my mind, no, probably not. Not if it’s the same kind of thing they’ve been showing me. What would change things is them actually telling the truth, saying where my money is, and stopping all the excuses. Until that happens, I have to keep confronting them, because nobody else is taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself distrusting people in general, not just those involved with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not everybody. I wouldn’t say I walk around distrusting every single person I see, if that’s what you mean. Like, if someone at a store gives me change or somebody says hello, I’m not sitting there thinking about them the same way.\n\nBut with this situation, it’s different, because it involves my money and the people who keep telling me not to question it. So maybe I’m more careful now. I do pay attention more. If somebody is too quick to say, “Oh, don’t worry about it,” or they start acting like I’m making a big deal out of nothing, then yes, I wonder why they’re saying that. Because that’s how people have been acting with me.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think it’s people in general exactly. It’s more that once I feel someone is covering something up or brushing me off, then I don’t trust them. And lately that has happened a lot around this money, so it probably looks like I don’t trust people, but I think I have a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself distrusting people in other areas of your life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of the money thing, I don’t sit there making a list of who I trust and who I don’t. I guess I notice people more now, like how they answer me, whether they’re trying to rush me or make me feel stupid for asking something. But I don’t know if that counts as distrusting people in other areas or just me being careful because of what’s been going on.\n\nLike, if someone is just talking about normal things, I can be normal with them. I’m not thinking every person is against me. But if they start acting like I’m supposed to just accept what they say and not ask questions, then I get that feeling again, like, why are you pushing so hard? Why can’t you just answer straight?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe I’m more guarded than I used to be, but I don’t really see it as a separate thing. It mostly comes back to people not being honest with me about my money and then making me feel like I’m wrong for noticing. Outside of that, I haven’t really thought about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think most people are generally honest with you in your daily life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer “most people.” That feels like a big question. I mean, day to day, if somebody is just doing their job or talking normal, I don’t automatically think, “This person is lying to me.” Like if someone at the store tells me the price of something, or someone says they’ll be there at a certain time, I usually just take it for what it is unless something feels off.\n\nBut I guess I pay more attention than I used to. If someone answers too fast, or they talk around something, or they act like I shouldn’t be asking, then I start wondering. I don’t know if that means I think people in general aren’t honest, or if I just don’t like being brushed aside. Because when people are honest, they can just answer plainly, right? They don’t have to make you feel confused or like you’re the problem.\n\nSo outside of the money situation… I haven’t really noticed everyone being dishonest, no. Not everyone. But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.\n\nSo if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. But the papers they keep putting in front of me aren’t convincing to me because I already know they can make things look one way. That’s what I’m saying. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.\n\nIt’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. I’m tired of having to keep saying it, but I can’t just drop it because they want me to. So, no, not just any “evidence” would change it. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your money being hidden is not based on real events?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s possible. I mean, I know people want me to say, “Maybe I misunderstood,” or “Maybe I’m seeing it wrong,” but that’s not what happened. This isn’t just some feeling I had one time. It’s been over a year of me seeing things not add up, and every time I ask, it turns into bank papers being shoved at me like that’s supposed to end the whole conversation.\n\nAnd that’s why I came in, really, because my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. They keep acting like if the paper says one thing, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know they’re hiding my money. They’re using bills, and fundraisers, and trip plans, and all these excuses to cover where it really is or where it went. It’s always, “Oh, this bill had to be paid,” or “That was for this thing,” or “We’re planning this trip,” like there’s always another reason I’m not allowed to see it straight.\n\nSo when you ask if it’s not based on real events… no. It is based on real things. I didn’t make this up. I wouldn’t keep confronting them if there wasn’t something there. I’m tired of having to keep asking, honestly, but I can’t just stop because they want me to. They need to tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. They don’t have to make you feel confused or like you’re the problem.\n\nSo outside of the money situation… I haven’t really noticed everyone being dishonest, no. Not everyone. But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.\n\nSo if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. But the papers they keep putting in front of me aren’t convincing to me because I already know they can make things look one way. That’s what I’m saying. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.\n\nIt’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. I’m tired of having to keep saying it, but I can’t just drop it because they want me to. So, no, not just any “evidence” would change it. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your money being hidden is not based on real events?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s possible. I mean, I know people want me to say, “Maybe I misunderstood,” or “Maybe I’m seeing it wrong,” but that’s not what happened. This isn’t just some feeling I had one time. It’s been over a year of me seeing things not add up, and every time I ask, it turns into bank papers being shoved at me like that’s supposed to end the whole conversation.\n\nAnd that’s why I came in, really, because my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. They keep acting like if the paper says one thing, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know they’re hiding my money. They’re using bills, and fundraisers, and trip plans, and all these excuses to cover where it really is or where it went. It’s always, “Oh, this bill had to be paid,” or “That was for this thing,” or “We’re planning this trip,” like there’s always another reason I’m not allowed to see it straight.\n\nSo when you ask if it’s not based on real events… no. It is based on real things. I didn’t make this up. I wouldn’t keep confronting them if there wasn’t something there. I’m tired of having to keep asking, honestly, but I can’t just stop because they want me to. They need to tell the truth.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know how I’m supposed to accept something that isn’t true. That’s the problem. People keep saying, “What if we show you proof?” but the proof they keep talking about is the same bank papers my family and friends already keep bringing up, and they say those papers prove nothing was taken. But they don’t prove that to me. They just prove what they want written down.

I came in because I’m tired of everybody acting like I’m supposed to stop asking questions. This has been going on for over a year. I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and every time I try to get a straight answer, there’s some other explanation—bills, fundraisers, trip plans, something else that makes it sound normal. But it’s like they use all that to cover it up, so nobody has to say where it really is.

So if you’re asking if I could ever accept it no matter what proof I’m shown… no, not if it’s that kind of proof. Not if it’s just more papers and more talking around it. I need them to tell the truth. I have to keep confronting them until they do, because if I stop, then they just get away with it, and everybody says, “See, it was fine.” But it’s not fine. Something happened with my money, and I know it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because everybody keeps acting like I’m making a big thing out of nothing, but I’m not. My family and my friends keep telling me the bank papers show nothing was taken, like I’m supposed to just look at papers and forget what I know. But I know my money is being hidden. It’s been going on for over a year now, and they keep trying to cover it up with all these stories about bills, or fundraisers, or trips they’re planning, like that explains where everything went.\n\nAnd I keep confronting them because what else am I supposed to do? They won’t just tell the truth. They act like I’m confused, but I’m not confused. I just need someone to understand that the papers don’t prove anything if the people handling them are hiding things. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is intentionally hiding your money from you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I know people keep telling me, “Look at the bank papers, nothing is missing,” but that’s exactly the point — the papers are what they keep using to make me look wrong. For over a year now, I’ve known my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just some feeling that came and went. It keeps happening, and every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s another excuse.\n\nOne time it’s bills, then it’s some fundraiser, then somebody is talking about a trip they’re planning, and it’s like all these stories show up right when I start asking where the money went. And my family and friends act like I’m being unreasonable because they can point to papers and say, “See?” But if the people handling it are the ones covering it up, then how am I supposed to trust that?\n\nSo yes, I think it’s intentional. I keep confronting them because nobody will just tell me the truth. They keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused about this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any doubts about your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, people keep asking me that like if I say I had one second where I wondered, then that means the whole thing isn’t real. But I know what I know. For over a year this has been happening, and every time I try to talk about my money, they pull out those bank papers like that settles everything.\n\nBut papers don’t mean anything if the people involved are hiding things. That’s what nobody listens to. They’ll say it went to bills, or then suddenly somebody brings up a fundraiser, or there’s some trip being planned, and it always comes up right when I start asking questions. It’s too much to be nothing.\n\nSo no, I don’t sit there thinking maybe I’m wrong. I came here because my family and my friends keep trying to make me doubt myself, but I’m not confused. I keep confronting them because I want the truth, and they keep acting like showing me papers is the truth. It isn’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are trying to harm you in any way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “harm” is the word everybody wants me to use, like physically hurt me or something, but it is hurting me, because hiding someone’s money from them is harm. Making me look like I’m confused when I’m not, that’s harm too.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but I keep telling them those papers don’t prove anything if the money is being hidden behind all these excuses. For over a year I’ve known this has been going on. Every time I ask, it turns into something else — bills, or some fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and then I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nSo maybe they’re not trying to hit me or anything like that, but they are trying to keep me from my own money and make me doubt myself. And I’m not going to just let that happen. That’s why I keep confronting them, because someone needs to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think that’s possible. I know that sounds stubborn to people, but I’ve been over this and over this for more than a year. I didn’t just wake up one day and decide it. I came in because my family and my friends keep saying, “Look, the bank papers prove nothing was taken,” but those papers are exactly what they keep hiding behind.\n\nEvery time I ask where my money is, it turns into some other explanation. First it’s bills, then somebody talks about a fundraiser, then there’s talk about trip plans, and it always comes up right when I start pressing them. That isn’t a coincidence to me. That’s covering it up.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’m mistaken. I think they want me to think I’m mistaken so I’ll stop asking. But I’m not going to stop, because it’s my money, and I need someone to tell me the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that other people, who are not involved in your family or friends, would agree that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I think if they really looked at it, yes, they would see something isn’t right. I mean, not if they just glance at the bank papers and do what everybody else does, where they say, “Well, the paper says this, so that’s the end of it.” That’s not really looking. That’s just accepting what my family and friends keep handing over.\n\nBecause I came here exactly because of that. They keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken, like that should shut me up, but I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. And every time I ask, there’s another story — bills, fundraisers, trip plans — always something that comes up to explain why the money isn’t where it should be or why I shouldn’t be asking so much.\n\nSo if someone outside of them actually listened and didn’t already take their side, I think they’d agree there’s a cover-up. Maybe some people wouldn’t want to get involved, or they’d just say it sounds like a family money issue, but that doesn’t mean I’m wrong. It just means they’re not paying close enough attention. That’s why I keep confronting them, because until somebody tells the truth, they’re just going to keep hiding behind papers and excuses.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your belief about your money being hidden might be incorrect, even if just for a moment?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a second, like when everybody keeps saying it over and over, I’ll think, “Well, what if I’m missing something?” But then I go right back over it in my head, because it doesn’t add up.\n\nI came in because my family and my friends keep pointing to the bank papers like that settles everything, like because the papers say nothing was taken then I’m supposed to just believe it. But I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not just a feeling out of nowhere. Every time I ask about it, suddenly there’s some other reason — bills, or a fundraiser, or somebody’s trip plans — and it’s always used to explain things away or make me stop asking.\n\nSo yes, maybe the thought crosses my mind for a moment because they keep pushing it on me, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think they want me to doubt myself so I’ll leave it alone. And I can’t do that, because it’s my money, and until they tell the truth, I have to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that your money is being hidden would change if you found out your family and friends were telling the truth about the bank papers?\n\nPatient: If they were really telling the truth? I mean… that’s the thing, doctor, I don’t think they are. They keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, but that’s exactly what I keep saying — they use those papers like a wall. Like once they show me some papers, I’m supposed to stop thinking and stop asking.\n\nI came here because this has been going on for over a year. I’ve known my money is being hidden from me, and every time I try to get a straight answer, it turns into something else. One time it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s somebody’s trip plans, and somehow all of that is supposed to explain why things don’t make sense. It always comes up when I start pressing them.\n\nSo if you’re asking would I change my mind just because they said the bank papers are true, no. Because that’s what they’ve already been saying. They’ve been saying it the whole time. And I still know something is wrong. I would need the truth, not just more papers and excuses. Until they actually admit where my money is and stop covering it up, I’m going to keep confronting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the people hiding your money are doing it for personal gain?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s for personal gain, or at least some kind of benefit to them. I mean, why else keep hiding it for over a year? Why else keep acting like the bank papers prove everything is fine when they know I’m asking for the truth?\n\nThey keep saying nothing was taken, nothing is wrong, but then every time I bring it up there’s another reason for where the money supposedly went or why I shouldn’t worry about it. First it’s bills, then it’s a fundraiser, then it’s trip plans, and it all sounds like excuses to cover what they’re doing. Maybe one person is using it, maybe more than one of them is, I don’t know exactly who got what, but I know they’re keeping it from me.\n\nAnd that’s what makes me so upset, because they act like I’m the problem for confronting them. But if they weren’t gaining something from it, they would just tell me the truth and show me where my money is without all the stories. So yes, I think somebody is benefiting, and until they admit it, I’m not going to stop asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any evidence that could change your mind about your money being hidden?\n\nPatient: I don’t know what kind of evidence you mean, because they keep saying they already have evidence with those bank papers, and that has not changed my mind. That’s what they keep doing — they hold up the papers and say, “See, nothing was taken,” like I’m supposed to just accept that and stop asking questions.\n\nBut I’ve known for over a year that my money is being hidden from me. It’s not like I woke up one day and decided it. Every time I ask, there’s always some reason ready — bills, or a fundraiser, or someone’s trip plans — and somehow I’m supposed to believe all of that explains it. It feels like they keep moving things around and covering it up, then acting like I’m wrong for noticing.\n\nSo if you mean would some paper change my mind, no, probably not. Not if it’s the same kind of thing they’ve been showing me. What would change things is them actually telling the truth, saying where my money is, and stopping all the excuses. Until that happens, I have to keep confronting them, because nobody else is taking it seriously.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself distrusting people in general, not just those involved with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, not everybody. I wouldn’t say I walk around distrusting every single person I see, if that’s what you mean. Like, if someone at a store gives me change or somebody says hello, I’m not sitting there thinking about them the same way.\n\nBut with this situation, it’s different, because it involves my money and the people who keep telling me not to question it. So maybe I’m more careful now. I do pay attention more. If somebody is too quick to say, “Oh, don’t worry about it,” or they start acting like I’m making a big deal out of nothing, then yes, I wonder why they’re saying that. Because that’s how people have been acting with me.\n\nSo I guess I’m not sure how to answer it. I don’t think it’s people in general exactly. It’s more that once I feel someone is covering something up or brushing me off, then I don’t trust them. And lately that has happened a lot around this money, so it probably looks like I don’t trust people, but I think I have a reason.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself distrusting people in other areas of your life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, outside of the money thing, I don’t sit there making a list of who I trust and who I don’t. I guess I notice people more now, like how they answer me, whether they’re trying to rush me or make me feel stupid for asking something. But I don’t know if that counts as distrusting people in other areas or just me being careful because of what’s been going on.\n\nLike, if someone is just talking about normal things, I can be normal with them. I’m not thinking every person is against me. But if they start acting like I’m supposed to just accept what they say and not ask questions, then I get that feeling again, like, why are you pushing so hard? Why can’t you just answer straight?\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe I’m more guarded than I used to be, but I don’t really see it as a separate thing. It mostly comes back to people not being honest with me about my money and then making me feel like I’m wrong for noticing. Outside of that, I haven’t really thought about it much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think most people are generally honest with you in your daily life, outside of this situation with your money?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer “most people.” That feels like a big question. I mean, day to day, if somebody is just doing their job or talking normal, I don’t automatically think, “This person is lying to me.” Like if someone at the store tells me the price of something, or someone says they’ll be there at a certain time, I usually just take it for what it is unless something feels off.\n\nBut I guess I pay more attention than I used to. If someone answers too fast, or they talk around something, or they act like I shouldn’t be asking, then I start wondering. I don’t know if that means I think people in general aren’t honest, or if I just don’t like being brushed aside. Because when people are honest, they can just answer plainly, right? They don’t have to make you feel confused or like you’re the problem.\n\nSo outside of the money situation… I haven’t really noticed everyone being dishonest, no. Not everyone. But I wouldn’t say I’m relaxed about it either. I guess I’m more careful now. I watch what people say and how they say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself questioning the honesty of people you've known for a long time, like childhood friends or family members not involved in the money issue?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the same way. Like, with family or people I’ve known a long time, if they’re not part of this money thing, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, they’re lying to me,” just because they’re talking to me. I mean, sometimes I wonder if people understand what I’m saying or if they’re just trying to calm me down, but that’s different, I think.\n\nChildhood friends… I don’t really have a lot of close ones I’m talking to all the time, so it’s hard to say. If someone I’ve known forever says something normal to me, I don’t automatically question every word. But if they start telling me I should drop it, or saying maybe I’m looking at it wrong, then I do get bothered, because then it feels like they’re taking the same side as everybody else, even if they’re not directly involved.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed myself questioning everyone from my past or all my family about everything. It’s more… if the conversation gets near this situation, or if they act like I’m supposed to just accept things, then I get guarded. Outside of that, I can still talk to them like normal, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you think any evidence, no matter how convincing, could ever change your belief that your money is being hidden from you?\n\nPatient: I mean… that’s hard, because everybody keeps saying “evidence” like I haven’t looked at anything, like I just woke up one day and decided this. My family and friends keep saying the bank papers prove nothing was taken, and that I should just accept that, but those papers don’t explain everything. They don’t explain why things don’t add up, or why money keeps getting talked around with bills, and fundraisers, and trips, like there’s always some other reason it has to be moved or used or not shown plainly.\n\nSo if you’re asking could something change my mind… I guess if someone actually told the truth and showed me where all of it went, every part of it, not just some paper they say proves it, then maybe. But the papers they keep putting in front of me aren’t convincing to me because I already know they can make things look one way. That’s what I’m saying. They’re hiding it, and then when I question it, they act like I’m the problem.\n\nIt’s been over a year of this, and I wouldn’t keep confronting them if I didn’t know something was wrong. I’m tired of having to keep saying it, but I can’t just drop it because they want me to. So, no, not just any “evidence” would change it. Not if it’s the same kind of bank papers and explanations that don’t answer what I’m asking. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your money being hidden is not based on real events?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s possible. I mean, I know people want me to say, “Maybe I misunderstood,” or “Maybe I’m seeing it wrong,” but that’s not what happened. This isn’t just some feeling I had one time. It’s been over a year of me seeing things not add up, and every time I ask, it turns into bank papers being shoved at me like that’s supposed to end the whole conversation.\n\nAnd that’s why I came in, really, because my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. They keep acting like if the paper says one thing, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know they’re hiding my money. They’re using bills, and fundraisers, and trip plans, and all these excuses to cover where it really is or where it went. It’s always, “Oh, this bill had to be paid,” or “That was for this thing,” or “We’re planning this trip,” like there’s always another reason I’m not allowed to see it straight.\n\nSo when you ask if it’s not based on real events… no. It is based on real things. I didn’t make this up. I wouldn’t keep confronting them if there wasn’t something there. I’m tired of having to keep asking, honestly, but I can’t just stop because they want me to. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever be able to accept that your money is not being hidden from you, no matter what proof you are shown?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how I’m supposed to accept something that isn’t true. That’s the problem. People keep saying, “What if we show you proof?” but the proof they keep talking about is the same bank papers my family and friends already keep bringing up, and they say those papers prove nothing was taken. But they don’t prove that to me. They just prove what they want written down.\n\nI came in because I’m tired of everybody acting like I’m supposed to stop asking questions. This has been going on for over a year. I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and every time I try to get a straight answer, there’s some other explanation—bills, fundraisers, trip plans, something else that makes it sound normal. But it’s like they use all that to cover it up, so nobody has to say where it really is.\n\nSo if you’re asking if I could ever accept it no matter what proof I’m shown… no, not if it’s that kind of proof. Not if it’s just more papers and more talking around it. I need them to tell the truth. I have to keep confronting them until they do, because if I stop, then they just get away with it, and everybody says, “See, it was fine.” But it’s not fine. Something happened with my money, and I know it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of your money being hidden is not based on real events?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think that’s possible. I mean, I know people want me to say, “Maybe I misunderstood,” or “Maybe I’m seeing it wrong,” but that’s not what happened. This isn’t just some feeling I had one time. It’s been over a year of me seeing things not add up, and every time I ask, it turns into bank papers being shoved at me like that’s supposed to end the whole conversation.\n\nAnd that’s why I came in, really, because my family and friends keep saying those bank papers prove nothing was taken. They keep acting like if the paper says one thing, then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know they’re hiding my money. They’re using bills, and fundraisers, and trip plans, and all these excuses to cover where it really is or where it went. It’s always, “Oh, this bill had to be paid,” or “That was for this thing,” or “We’re planning this trip,” like there’s always another reason I’m not allowed to see it straight.\n\nSo when you ask if it’s not based on real events… no. It is based on real things. I didn’t make this up. I wouldn’t keep confronting them if there wasn’t something there. I’m tired of having to keep asking, honestly, but I can’t just stop because they want me to. They need to tell the truth.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would ever be able to accept that your money is not being hidden from you, no matter what proof you are shown?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how I’m supposed to accept something that isn’t true. That’s the problem. People keep saying, “What if we show you proof?” but the proof they keep talking about is the same bank papers my family and friends already keep bringing up, and they say those papers prove nothing was taken. But they don’t prove that to me. They just prove what they want written down.\n\nI came in because I’m tired of everybody acting like I’m supposed to stop asking questions. This has been going on for over a year. I’ve known they’re hiding my money, and every time I try to get a straight answer, there’s some other explanation—bills, fundraisers, trip plans, something else that makes it sound normal. But it’s like they use all that to cover it up, so nobody has to say where it really is.\n\nSo if you’re asking if I could ever accept it no matter what proof I’m shown… no, not if it’s that kind of proof. Not if it’s just more papers and more talking around it. I need them to tell the truth. I have to keep confronting them until they do, because if I stop, then they just get away with it, and everybody says, “See, it was fine.” But it’s not fine. Something happened with my money, and I know it."
  }
]
